- Four spaces were insufficient between the shared `예` and `아니요`; six spaces are the current candidate and move the negative option two cells farther right without changing cursor coordinates.
- The low-code `0x6C` plane is shared by equipment `LV +1` and battle `LV1`. v0.34 uses a heavier 30-pixel L/V bitmap while preserving every neighboring plane.

Shared confirmation-box geometry - 2026-07-18
- The shared confirmation renderer begins at RAM `0x80160B8C`. It reads the `예      아니요` pointer from `PSX.EXE 0x82AC0` and stores the two selector X positions after drawing the option row.
- The first selector position is `s1 - 4`; the prompt is drawn at `box_x + 8`. Moving the selector right independently risks overlapping `예` and must not be used as the first repair.
- The box width instruction is `PSX.EXE 0x463D8` / RAM `0x80160BD8`, originally `addiu s5, v1, 0x10`. The prompt X instruction is `PSX.EXE 0x46428` / RAM `0x80160C28`, originally `addiu a0, s2, 8`.
- v0.37 changes the width margin from 16 to 40 pixels and the prompt inset from 8 to 20 pixels. Automatic centering moves the box left by 12 pixels; the inset compensation keeps the prompt at its previous absolute X. The option row and both selector positions are mathematically unchanged, so the only visual change is 12 pixels of extra box area on each side.
- Build future confirmation geometry from the hash-locked v0.36 ZIP. Re-running the cumulative string-pool builder is not byte-stable across inherited mutable module state and caused an automatically rejected 385-byte executable delta.

Accepted compact-LV source - 2026-07-18
- The user runtime-approved equipment text `던지기 LV +1` uses the v0.34 30-pixel low-code `0x6C` bitmap. Preserve `COMM.IMG` SHA256 `FB6D4027023C6A75A1561D72507C52656472B4F31E1EB92B73965CA3B51543EA` until a separately verified glyph expansion replaces the level wording.

Safe same-page E7 icon relocation - 2026-07-18
- Font rows 10-11, columns 0-2 correspond to 24 codes `DF6F-DF7A` and `DFC3-DFCE`. None is assigned in `korean_charmap.csv` or `korean_charmap_extended.csv`, and none occurs in the current active story/UI payload audit.
- Therefore icon IDs 2 and 3 can use same-page destinations `(0,130)` and `(12,130)`. Copy the exact duplicates from `(114,354)` and `(162,354)`, then change only U bytes at `PSX.EXE 0x80214/0x80216` to `0x00/0x0C`.
- Keep fixed V `0x82`, width 12, height 12, CLUT, and renderer code unchanged. This avoids both the damaged original rectangles and an unverified texture-page switch.
- The v0.38 bottom payload at `0x82094` is `E7 02 + 결정 + space + E7 03 + 돌아가기`; pointer word `0x8235C` selects it.

v0.38 LV preference - 2026-07-18
- The user explicitly preferred the v0.35 V shape over the v0.34 heavy shape. v0.38 restores the exact v0.35 23-pixel bitmap as a runtime candidate; do not alter any neighboring plane.

Battle HUD label pointer correction - 2026-07-18
- Correct the earlier broad statement that item help and the complete battle HUD use only shared code `0x6C`. Item descriptions use compact `0x6C`, but the dynamic battle HUD row has five additional hardcoded label pointers at `PSX.EXE 0x823AC`, `0x823B0`, `0x823B4`, `0x823B8`, and `0x823BC`.
- In the clean executable they target four-byte slots at `0x82390`, `0x82394`, `0x82398`, `0x8239C`, and `0x823A0`. v0.35 reclaimed that pool for Korean strings without repointing these consumers, so battle runtime interpreted Korean string tails as L/V/M/P labels.
- GPU sprite packets extracted from the user's v0.38 savestate reproduce the corrupt row and prove this pointer family is active. The proof image is `01_work/analysis/ui_v39_savestate_2026-07-18/grp1bf_font_packets.bmp`.
- Safe repair: store `6C 00 00 00`, `00 00 00 00`, `DD B2 00 00`, `01 DE 4F 00`, and `DD 90 00 00` at `0x820A8-0x820BB`, then repoint the five words. This preserves compact LV and the original auxiliary/M/P planes.

E7_02 U=0 boundary failure - 2026-07-18
- v0.38 runtime proves E7_03 at U=0x0C works while E7_02 at U=0x00 is corrupt even though its copied bitmap is statically correct. Treat U=0 as unsafe for this renderer path.
- Same-page column 2 at `(24,130)`, U=0x18, is unassigned in both Korean charmaps. v0.39 places the verified circle duplicate there, restores abandoned `(0,130)` from v0.37, and preserves the working X rectangle byte-for-byte.

v0.39 frozen rollback baseline - 2026-07-18
- The exact rollback artifact is `99_backup/baselines/ui_safe_v39_fallback_2026-07-18/ui_safe_v39_cumulative_patch_only.zip`, SHA256 `0778FE435820409F190579D179F8B36FFFCEB02B5F2004FC1E3ACE58741D5DC3`.
- Preserve this baseline while investigating a physically independent UI/battle glyph atlas. Do not reuse its ZIP name for experiments.
- Runtime-known defects are the battle-HUD `LV`, E7_02 circle/confirm icon, and a dark unexpected mark rendered inside the battle cursor. Treat all three as mandatory canaries for future texture/VRAM work.

S3023 untranslated duplicate-scene fact - 2026-07-18
- `31/S3023.DAT` is present in the v0.39 cumulative ZIP; it was not missing from packaging.
- The high-confidence body at file offset `0x47E4C`, capacity 39, remains byte-identical to the Japanese original and is classified `original` in `01_work/analysis/full_audit_v20/story_body_audit.csv`.
- Its source is `兵士 / カカラスの森へのモンスターだったら / おまえらでも勝てるだろ？`. This is a translation-manifest coverage omission distinct from the translated `S3031` general-room scene.

Sparse COMM glyph-store mapping - 2026-07-18
- The COMM texture begins in sampled runtime VRAM at halfword X=44. A logical 12x12 COMM cell `(row, col)` maps to source X=`col*12` and runtime VRAM X=`44 + col*3`, Y=`row*12`.
- The rejected v0.26 experiment allocated physical glyph indices 1240-1629 as one continuous range. That range crosses active character and battle graphics, explaining the runtime sprite corruption, long battle entry, empty accessory list, and lock. Do not reuse continuous index allocation.
- Comparing v0.39 `COMM.IMG` with 55 DuckStation savestates found 161 universally zero cells. The v0.40 probe conservatively selects 159 cells from rows 14 and above and excludes the unrelated low cells.
- Those selected cells expose 384 independently addressable E1-E8 bitplanes. Physical storage is sparse; code assignment must come from `05_docs/ui_glyph_store_v40_map.csv`, never from arithmetic assumptions about a continuous atlas.
- Current translated UI payloads use 280 unique Hangul syllables. `덕` and `량` use existing one-byte glyphs and remain there because replacing them with two-byte sparse codes would change fixed string lengths. The other 278 syllables move to sparse E1-E8 codes, leaving 106 verified planes free.
- This route requires no loader, texture-page, pointer-table, or string-pool expansion. It changes glyph bitplanes plus existing string codes in place, so pointer words and encoded lengths remain stable.
- Protected canaries for every future sparse-bank build are the v0.39 `0x6C` LV plane, E7 icon rectangles, battle-cursor region, five HUD special payloads, 17 Japanese-preserved rows, and all story members.
- v0.40 is a runtime probe, not an accepted baseline. Static success does not prove that every selected sparse cell stays unused in unsampled late-game scenes; preserve v0.39 for immediate rollback until broad runtime verification.

Dynamic UI control-prefix collision - 2026-07-18
- Correct the v0.40 assumption that every prefix accepted by `0x8016B3C0` is directly usable as a UI glyph. The decoder accepts high prefixes, but the dynamic parser intercepts commands first.
- At `0x8016BB48`, the pre-processing loop sends a pending byte at or above `E1` to `0x8016BBE4`. At `0x8016BB74`, the main loop sends a current state at or above `E1` to `0x8016BD48` instead of the glyph decoder.
- `0x8016BBE4-0x8016BCD0` explicitly handles `E1`, `E2`, `E6`, and `E8`; the jump table at `0x8016BD48` covers the `E2-E8` control family. Therefore `E1/E2` must never be assigned as glyph prefixes for dynamic UI strings.
- This explains all v0.40 runtime symptoms: text color/state fragments, blank labels, block graphics, and a progression stop. The sparse COMM storage itself read back correctly in slots 1-4.
- A safe extension must allocate a new namespace rather than overload controls. `E9/EA` do not occur at character boundaries in the 503 current UI records and were invalid in the original dynamic parser, making them suitable after an explicit parser patch.
- The decoder must map `E9/EA` through a 278-entry 16-bit table to the verified sparse physical indices; formula-based continuous allocation would reintroduce v0.26 graphics collisions.
- The loaded, zero, unreferenced cave `PSX.EXE 0x8CC60-0x8D05F` can hold the lookup table and MIPS classifier/decoder stubs. Patch both parser classification sites, not only the decoder.

E9/EA UI namespace implementation - 2026-07-18
- E9 01-FE provides 254 virtual glyph IDs and EA 01-18 provides 24 more, exactly covering the 278 sparse UI Hangul glyphs.
- The physical lookup index is `(second - 1)` for E9 and `254 + (second - 1)` for EA. A 278-entry little-endian 16-bit table maps that logical ID to the verified sparse COMM physical index.
- The v0.41 code layout is: pre-classifier 0x801A7460 (44 bytes), main classifier 0x801A748C (44 bytes), decoder 0x801A74B8 (104 bytes), table 0x801A7520 (556 bytes), used end 0x801A774C.
- Hooks replace only two instructions each at 0x8016BB48, 0x8016BB74, and 0x8016B3D4. Original E1-E8 classification remains unchanged for every byte except E9/EA.
- All 2,857 story bodies included in the cumulative v0.39 archive have zero E9/EA occurrences at decoded character boundaries. Raw E9/EA second bytes inside DD-E0 glyphs are harmless and must not be mistaken for prefix collisions.
- Do not reuse E1-E8 as UI glyph prefixes. Do not replace the sparse physical table with a continuous formula.

E9/EA runtime confirmation - 2026-07-18
- The three-hook E9/EA design is confirmed at runtime: glyph lookup, rendering, input, and parser continuation all work.
- A visible defect in the same screen does not automatically implicate the new glyph store. Compare against v0.39 before changing hooks or lookup data.
- Known v0.39 defects still present in v0.41 are the compact HUD LV glyph, affected confirm/cancel icon cells, and battle target cursor artifact.
- Future UI fixes must build on the frozen v0.41 runtime baseline and must not remove the E9/EA hooks, sparse lookup table, or story E2 mechanism.

v0.42 sparse-store and inherited-defect findings - 2026-07-18
- The 636 runtime-verified sparse physical glyph planes are valid lookup targets even when their old direct carrier bytes are not valid E1-E8 character codes. E9/EA lookup returns the physical index directly, so new assignments must use the verified physical-index set rather than reconstructing legacy direct codes.
- v0.42 uses 409 lookup entries: 278 preserved v0.41 assignments and 131 new sparse glyphs. The table occupies 818 bytes from 0x801A7520 through 0x801A7851 and leaves 14 bytes in the loaded executable cave.
- Repacked UI strings use 429 unique payloads across 503 pointer records and leave 733 bytes free after reserving 0x82134-0x8216F for the E7 V-coordinate stub and HUD payloads.
- E7 confirm/cancel icons cannot share the old fixed V=130 renderer row with the relocated clean pixels. Icon IDs 2 and 3 now use U=180/192 and V=228 through a dedicated hook at RAM 0x8019C934; other E7 icons retain their original V behavior.
- The battle target cursor artifact was inherited from v0.38/v0.39 COMM edits. Restoring x=0..35, y=128..159 exactly from v0.37 removes that pollution without touching unrelated glyph planes.
- The HUD LV defect must not be repaired by another compact one-cell substitute. v0.42 allocates separate sparse L and V glyphs and preserves the original M and P cells.
- Do not reintroduce a continuous font-bank formula, overwrite the accepted E9/EA hooks, or reuse the v0.39 compact LV/icon cells in later UI work.

v0.42 shared-pool orphaning and v0.43 recovery - 2026-07-18
- `build_ui_guide_repairs_v42.allocate_pool()` clears every range in the nine shared UI string regions before repacking only the 503 audited table records.
- The executable has additional common/system/world pointers into those same regions. Comparing v0.39 manifests against v0.42 found 71 non-table mismatches.
- One mismatch at pointer `0x80F1C` is intentionally superseded by a v0.42 audited consumable-table record. One is the separately handled HUD family. The remaining 69 pointers require restoration.
- Common strings are zero-terminated byte streams containing mixed one-byte and two-byte glyph codes. Their boundary is the first `00` byte, not an aligned `00 00` word.
- v0.43 packs 58 unique recovery payloads into holes left around the immutable 503 v0.42 records. It consumes 693 of 733 available bytes and leaves 40 bytes.
- Configuration pointer `0x825D8` is a layout-specific exception. The full Korean phrase exceeds the value-column budget; `몬스터 도감` uses existing v0.42 virtual glyphs and requires no COMM change.
- v0.42 separate sparse L/V HUD payloads failed in runtime presentation. v0.43 deliberately uses the accepted compact v0.39 HUD payload as a recovery fallback until a renderer-specific two-cell HUD repair is proven.
- v0.43 does not change `COMM.IMG`; therefore no icon claim may be inferred from this build. Confirm/cancel and cursor behavior remain whatever v0.42 provides and require runtime verification.

Battle HUD LV and E7 index findings - 2026-07-19
- The active battle-skill HUD does not render L and V through two independent pointer payloads. Its first pointer must contain the combined two-glyph `LV` string; the second pointer is not the visible V path in the tested HUD.
- Do not restore the failed v0.42 separate-pointer L/V layout. A valid combined payload is `EA 9A EA 9B 00`, using the accepted E9/EA sparse glyph namespace.
- Runtime GPU packets and table simulation confirm that E7 control values are effectively one-based for this lookup: `E7 02` selects table index 1 and `E7 03` selects index 2. The previous assumption that they selected indices 2/3 was off by one.
- In the current `COMM.IMG`, the clean decision icon is already at U=192,V=228 and the clean X/back icon is already at U=180,V=228. Repair routing in `PSX.EXE`; do not copy or redraw these pixels again.
- The E7 V-coordinate hook must special-case table indices 1 and 2 only. Do not retarget indices 2 and 3, and do not alter unrelated E7 table entries while repairing these two icons.

v0.69 item-name leaf failure and v0.70 repair - 2026-07-22
- v0.69's manual leaf bitmap is stored behind virtual code `E9 75`, but only the two item-acquisition sentences referenced that code.
- The item-name table still used `EA 66` at `PSX.EXE 0x80A81` (`마력의 잎`) and `0x80A91` (`쓴 잎`). This split route is the confirmed reason the acquisition text and item popup could render differently.
- Repair item names by changing only those two code pairs to `E9 75`. Do not redraw `COMM.IMG` or modify unrelated glyph mappings for this defect.
- The active battle-help record at pointer `0x8234C` owns a 32-byte slot at `PSX.EXE 0x82780-0x8279F`. A replacement payload up to 31 bytes plus NUL fits without moving the pointer or touching the next string.
- `뉴` is absent from the accepted v0.42/v0.69 UI glyph map. Use the semantically equivalent existing-glyph wording `명령 링 열기` rather than extending the store during this narrow repair.

v0.70 leaf appearance and v0.71 bitmap repair - 2026-07-22
- v0.70 proves that both item-name records can safely reference the dedicated `E9 75` leaf code, but it also exposes a separate visual defect in v0.69's manually drawn leaf bitmap.
- The manual bitmap uses heavy two-pixel strokes and does not match the established UI Hangul. Do not preserve or reuse it as the final leaf glyph.
- The accepted UI/story Korean renderer is Gulim 12px centered in a 24x24 canvas, cropped to 12x12, and thresholded at 192 to one bit. v0.71 applies that renderer only to physical index 1356, row 16, column 3, plane 0.
- `링 열기` uses only existing accepted glyphs and occupies 18 bytes plus NUL in the 32-byte battle-help slot. It is the compact wording to use unless a future accepted store explicitly adds `뉴`.

Common UI high-page renderer findings - 2026-07-22
- The common UI glyph builder stores V as one byte. Physical rows 22 and above wrap their V coordinate while their bitmap data remains on texture page y=256.
- Do not relocate those glyph planes into visually empty low-page `COMM.IMG` cells. v0.72 proved that those cells can belong to battle sprites even when a UI-only inspection suggests they are unused.
- The live v0.71 lookup has 409 records, 57 row-22+ mappings, and no row-21 mapping. The design CSV is not authoritative for the final runtime count because v0.71 already repointed several entries to low rows.
- The safe candidate approach is a two-pass common-text renderer: low glyphs under the original tpage, then high glyphs under the same tpage with bit `0x10` set for y=256. Use a reserved per-state packet for double-buffer safety.
- Preserve explicit R3000 load-delay spacing in injected MIPS. A static load/use audit is mandatory before producing a runtime candidate.
- v0.73 implements this without changing `COMM.IMG`, DAT files, item strings, icon routes, or existing glyph pixels. Runtime acceptance is still required.

v0.73 leaf-function RA failure - 2026-07-22
- The common glyph builder at `0x8016B518` is a leaf function and does not save RA. Never insert `JAL` into this function unless the original caller RA is explicitly preserved.
- v0.73 hooked `0x8016B5D8` with `JAL 0x801A2094`. The helper returned to `0x8016B5E0`, but the glyph builder later executed its original `JR RA` with RA still equal to `0x8016B5E0`, creating an infinite loop through its own tail.
- DuckStation slot `HASH-DF8E4CEB26B8ECFB_1.sav` proves the failure: PC `0x8016B60C`, RA `0x8016B5E0`. The injected cave and all three v0.73 hooks are present intact in RAM, so this is not stale media or cave overwrite.
- The earlier `0x1A62` RAM-start claim for this state family was later disproved by an exact PSX.EXE payload-signature audit. For the latest inspected states, PS1 RAM begins at `0x10CA62`. Always rediscover or validate the boundary per state format.
- The safe trampoline is `J 0x801A2094` at the hook and `J 0x8016B5E0` at the helper tail. Plain `J` preserves the caller RA while retaining the displaced `LBU` and high-page flag logic.
- v0.74 applies only those two instruction-word repairs over exact v0.73. Runtime acceptance is still required; retain v0.71 as rollback.
- Do not use the captured v0.73 slot as a direct v0.74 runtime test. DuckStation savestates restore executable RAM, so loading that slot restores the bad v0.73 hook and helper even from a v0.74 disc. Use a cold boot/memory-card route or create a separately repaired savestate copy.

P6 renderer marker isolation - 2026-07-28
- v81's use of glyph-record byte `0x2B` as a P6 marker is invalid because the live game uses its high bit as part of the texture-page state, including the red ARC transition.
- Moving the marker to another glyph-record padding byte, as attempted by v82, does not establish ownership and remains unsafe.
- v83 records P6 membership outside game glyph records: one active text-state pointer plus two 32-bit glyph bitmaps in an executable-side sidecar.
- The glyph builder can classify P6 directly while parsing rows `0x18-0x1F`; it shifts only U by `0x28`, records the current glyph index externally, and preserves caller RA with a non-linking `J` trampoline.
- The renderer determines pass membership from the sidecar bitmap and leaves the original record tpage byte unchanged.
- Current v83 is a diagnostic limited to one active state and 64 glyphs. Runtime success must be established before generalizing it to multiple concurrent states.

P6 sidecar startup-clear overlap - 2026-07-28
- The PS-X EXE startup loop begins zeroing BSS at `0x801A86E8` and continues beyond the executable-loaded end.
- v83 placed its glyph helper at `0x801A86EC`, marker helper at `0x801A8788`, and sidecar at `0x801A87F4-0x801A87FF`. Startup therefore erased all three before first use.
- DuckStation slot `HASH-FD57765860D7D8B9_1.sav` proves this: all v83 hooks and the main renderer are present, the helper region is zero, and PC/next-PC are `0x801A8730`/`0x801A8734` inside the erased helper.
- Do not place executable code or persistent sidecar data in `0x801A86E8-0x801A87FF` while retaining the original BSS clear start.
- v84 changes the clear start to `0x801A8800`, which is both the PS-X EXE loaded end and the byte immediately after the reserved sidecar. The original bytes in the newly preserved tail were zero before v83 injection.
- This is a two-byte immediate repair over exact v83; no renderer, hook, DAT, COMM.IMG, story, icon, or item data changes.
- A savestate created under v83 cannot verify v84 directly because DuckStation restores the already erased executable RAM and stopped CPU state.

P6 high-RAM bootstrap relocation - 2026-07-28
- v84 proves that excluding the EXE tail from startup clearing is not sufficient. Runtime code later treats `0x801A86E8-0x801A87FF` as live BSS and overwrites the helper/sidecar. Never use this range as a persistent executable cave.
- The original BSS ends at `0x801FE3C4`; the PS-X EXE header initializes SP to `0x801FFFF0`. Across 97 valid saved CPU contexts, the lowest observed SP is `0x801FFE78`.
- v85 temporarily stores a 276-byte position-adjusted P6 image in the loaded EXE tail, copies it at entry to `0x801FE3C4-0x801FE4D7`, then clears the entire original BSS. The temporary source is intentionally erased; the relocated destination is above the clear endpoint.
- The high layout is glyph helper `0x801FE3C4`, marker helper `0x801FE460`, sidecar `0x801FE4CC-0x801FE4D7`. The observed minimum stack margin is 6,560 bytes.
- The entry bootstrap occupies exactly `0x801757BC-0x801757F3`. It calls BIOS A(2Ah) `memcpy`, which returns the destination in `v0`, uses that value as the BSS clear endpoint, restores `v0=4`, and leaves the original code from `0x801757F4` onward byte-identical.
- `0x801757B4` is a live JAL target and must not be overwritten while expanding the bootstrap backward.
- v85 does not grow `PSX.EXE` or alter ISO layout. Its runtime status remains unverified until both v84 freeze points progress normally.

P6 row-24 glyph repack - 2026-07-28
- The v85 lookup contains 57 active high-row mappings on physical rows 31, 32, 33, 38, and 39.
- P6 absolute texture area x=1576..1755, y=288..299 contains fifteen completely blank 12x12 cells across four bitplanes, enough for all 57 mappings.
- Repack destinations are physical indices 2016..2072, all on physical row 24. The helper must classify row 24 only (`sltiu` span 1), not rows 24..31.
- Copy each source bitplane independently and preserve the other three destination bitplanes. Treating the whole 4-bit cell as one glyph incorrectly flags later planes in the same cell as occupied.
- Do not reuse apparently blank low-page cells. v72 proved that they overlap battle sprites.
- `32/S3051.DAT` is absent from the 78-member v85 ZIP. The current work copy at `01_work/32/S3051.DAT` is SHA256 `A6DC6BD40F49526C39EE20D9C1990DA45860190F31F5E938E6039AFCF7C4BB1A` and must be added as a new cumulative patch member when patching that scene.

P6 high-RAM helper and InitHeap collision - 2026-07-29
- Placing persistent code immediately at the original BSS end is not sufficient. Startup uses that same boundary to initialize the heap.
- At `0x80175810`, `addiu a0,a0,-0x1c3c` forms `0x801FE3C4`. The delay-slot instruction at `0x8017584C` adds four, so BIOS A(39h) `InitHeap` receives `0x801FE3C8`.
- v85/v86 placed the helper at `0x801FE3C4`; therefore the heap header overwrote its second instruction at `0x801FE3C8`. Slots 1-4 all contain zero there while the source helper and surrounding P6 data remain valid.
- Reserve the whole helper image by changing the boundary to `0x801FE4D8`, its exclusive end. This makes the actual heap start `0x801FE4DC` and reduces heap size by exactly `0x114`, without moving its upper boundary.
- Do not solve this by moving the helper into the old EXE-tail BSS, low-page texture cells, or observed stack space. Those approaches were already rejected.

Current DuckStation savestate RAM layout - 2026-07-29
- Do not use the older hard-coded decompressed offsets `0x1262` or `0x1A62` for the latest inspected states.
- Pure 2 MiB PS1 RAM begins at decompressed offset `0x10CA62`. The exact PSX.EXE payload beginning at file offset `0x800` appears at state offset `0x11CA62`, corresponding to load address `0x00010000`.
- State-analysis scripts must discover or validate the RAM boundary from signatures before reading pointers or sidecars.

P6 sidecar lifetime across interleaved UI strings - 2026-07-29
- v87 fixed the InitHeap collision but did not fix P6 state lifetime.
- The helper had one active pointer/bitmap sidecar and cleared it whenever any new text state was parsed. A non-P6 UI string appearing between P6 parsing and rendering therefore erased the pending P6 bitmap.
- In the inspected item state, the sidecar retained active pointer `0x801F031C` but its bitmap words were zero. This matches the observed fallback to malformed P0 glyphs for the P6-only item characters.
- Non-P6 parsing must not clear pending P6 state. Only an actual P6 string may refresh the sidecar; renderer and marker pointer-equality guards must remain enabled.

Story and UI glyph namespaces must remain separate - 2026-07-29
- The S3032 story E2 encoder must use only the story charmap and normal/DD-E0 story glyph codes.
- Do not fall back to `ui_glyph_store_v42_map.csv` while encoding story text. UI virtual codes in the E9/EA namespace are not valid story glyphs and can be interpreted as controls.
- v88 encoded `책` as UI code `EA 6F`; runtime stopped exactly at that boundary after displaying `저희는`.
- Story code `E0 2D` renders as `듯` in the current runtime. The v89 attempt to reuse it as `책` did not provide a safe runtime glyph and caused `책임질` to display as `듯임질`. Do not use `E0 2D` for `책`.
- `야군` is the correct Korean transcription of character name `ヤクン`; it is not a corrupted form of `장군`.

S3032 E2 slot mapping and v90 rejection - 2026-07-29
- For the loaded S3032 block, `RAM offset = DAT file offset - 0x3C000`.
- File calls `0x47994`, `0x479EE`, and `0x47A40` reference E2 IDs 81, 82, and 83. Their 128-byte slots are `0x45080`, `0x45100`, and `0x45180`, mapping to RAM `0x9080`, `0x9100`, and `0x9180`.
- SUPERSEDED 2026-07-31 by "S3032 dialogue separators and the S2021 reference convention". `E6 01` is NOT a line break in these payloads; runtime screenshots show it rendering as a garbled glyph. Do not reintroduce it.
- The v90 same-object/non-P6 sidecar clear was also wrong. Item-name rendering reuses the same object; clearing at index zero removed a valid pending bitmap and produced a blank item-name window followed by a freeze.
- Latest slot 5 showed pointer `0x801F1DB4`, bitmap `0x10`, index zero under v90; the corresponding v89 state retained bitmap `0x12`. Restore the v89 helper before further lifetime experiments.
- v91 therefore restores v89 `PSX.EXE` and changes only S3032 E2 slot 2. Do not promote it until runtime verifies both item selection and dialogue advancement.

P6 pass classification must be per packet, not global state - 2026-07-31
- The glyph packet array is a 52-byte-stride array shared by the common glyph builder at `0x8016B518` and the E7 icon builder at `0x8016B668`. Both write U at `+0x28`, V at `+0x29`, W at `+0x2A`, H at `+0x2B`, X at `+0x2C`, Y at `+0x2E`, and CLUT at `+0x30`. Bytes `+0x32` and `+0x33` are spare.
- Each packet also carries two 20-byte `SPRT` primitives at `+0x00` and `+0x14`; the driver selects one with `s4 = framebuffer_parity * 20`.
- `SPRT` has no tpage field. High-page selection is a GPU state change: the driver at `0x801A2188` reads the base tpage from `0x801F2FFC`, masks with `0xFFE0`, sets bit `0x10` (ty=256), and builds a `DR_TPAGE`. Rendering is therefore two passes over the same array, and the marker value is only a pass discriminator, not a tpage value.
- The v83-v91 marker helper returned 0 whenever its single stored text-state pointer differed from the caller's. With several concurrent text objects, every glyph of a non-active object was classified low-page and drawn from the wrong texture page. This is the mechanism behind the malformed item-name glyphs.
- Do not try to fix this by changing when the sidecar is cleared. v88 (never clear) and v90 (clear on same object) both failed because one global slot cannot describe N concurrent text objects.
- The classification input is already per glyph. `0x8016B5A8` stores `V = row * 12` with `sb`, so row 24 truncates to 32, and `24*12 - 256 = 32` is simultaneously the correct within-high-page V. Among valid rows 0-42, only row 24 yields V == 32.
- v92 therefore tests `packet[0x29] == 32` inline and uses pass tags 0 and 1 instead of 0 and `0x80`, which avoids a shift instruction and keeps the loop body at its original addresses.
- Do not generalize this to `V % 12 != 0`. E7 icons use V = 130, which is not a multiple of 12 and would be misclassified as high page. Exact comparison against 32 is safe because 130 and 228 are both different from 32.
- The `U += 40` applied by the glyph helper is required for P6 cells and must be preserved by any replacement.
- To exceed row 24's 84 P6 slots, do not add more rows to an equality test. Have the builder write a flag into spare packet byte `+0x32` and have the renderer read that instead; this stays stateless and works for any row.

S3032 dialogue separators and the S2021 reference convention - 2026-07-31
- The working reference for a Korean dialogue payload is `21/S2021.DAT` at `0x45E00`: `E0 9C E0 9D DF 80 9C E0 68 ...` decoding to `아크: 우리가 ...`.
- That proven payload contains no `E6 01` anywhere. The speaker separator is the ordinary colon glyph `DF 80` followed by space `9C`, and line breaks are produced by the game's automatic word wrap, not by any control code.
- The four S3032 E2 dialogue blocks at `0x45000`, `0x45080`, `0x45100`, and `0x45180` each carried two `E6 01` codes. Runtime screenshots of blocks 1-3 show a garbled glyph at exactly those positions while every Korean syllable around them renders correctly and the scene advances normally.
- `E6 01` does appear in the original Japanese script region, for example at `0x47998`. Copying it into a Korean E2 payload is invalid; it resolves to no valid glyph there.
- The earlier claim that removing `E6 01` in v90 concatenated the lines is not supported. The lines were already continuous, and the "still malformed" v90 observation was made on blocks 1-2, which v90 never modified. v90 changed block 3 only.
- Diagnostic rule: before judging a dialogue repair, confirm that the on-screen text is the block that was actually edited. Blocks 1-3 all begin with `야군` and are easy to confuse.
- Dialogue windows come in three kinds: named speaker, speakerless, and portrait-plus-name. Only a named-speaker block takes the `DF 80 9C` colon separator. Block 4 of S3032 is speakerless, so both of its separators become plain spaces.
- The trailing byte of each 128-byte block is not a hard capacity limit. Block 3 measures 35 parser units against a trailing byte of `0x20` (32) and still renders correctly at runtime. Do not treat that byte as an allocation bound.
- Colon `DF 80` originates from the accepted `intro E2 expanded v0.1` work, so it is a runtime-proven story glyph rather than a new assignment.

Injected renderer entry point and the common-path violation - 2026-07-31
- The original game text renderer begins at `0x8016B764`. v73 replaced its first two instructions with `j 0x801A22A0`, which is the entry of the injected two-pass driver.
- v70, v71 and v72 hold the untouched originals: `0x8016B764 = 27BDFFD0` (`addiu sp,sp,-48`), `0x8016B768 = AFBF002C` (`sw ra,0x2C(sp)`), and `0x8016B5D8 = 90C2000E` (`lbu v0,0xE(a2)`). The whole injected region `0x801A20B0-0x801A22E0` is zero in those builds.
- Restoring those three words is the complete off-switch for the P6 rendering path. The injected driver, two-pass loop, marker helper and sidecar all become unreachable.
- The P6 glyph hook at `0x8016B5D8` sits 192 bytes inside the COMMON glyph sprite builder `0x8016B518`, which has five call sites and serves dialogue, item names, load/save screens, HUD and the E7 icon builder alike. The E7 icon builder writes the same 52-byte packets with the same U/V fields.
- This violates the design principle recorded when the separate storage was approved: only E2 expanded dialogue may use the new renderer, and the common text objects, sizes, terminators, pointers and rendering code must not be modified. Item names are not E2 dialogue yet they use 56 row-24 glyph codes, so they pass through the hooked common builder.
- This also explains the historical symptom migration, where repairing dialogue progress broke the transition screen and repairing that broke the load screen.
- v74 classified P6 membership by setting bit `0x80` inside packet byte `0x2B`, which is the sprite HEIGHT field. The renderer copies that byte straight into the SPRT height, so a flagged glyph asked the GPU for a 140-pixel-tall sprite. Do not reuse packet `0x2B` for flags.

P6 storage location is already runtime-validated - 2026-07-31
- The v78/v79/v80 probe series wrote distinguishable test patterns into three candidate VRAM regions and checked them on real hardware paths.
- P3-H contaminated the top of the load screen and P5-C contaminated the Sony screen; both are rejected.
- P6-D passed the Sony screen, title, load screen, field, menus, dialogue, battle and the red ARC transition with no contamination.
- Therefore the question of whether a separate glyph store is possible is already answered yes. The unresolved part is the rendering plumbing, not the storage.
- Do not treat glyph-store expansion or the P6 location as candidates for abandonment. The safe low-page area (rows 14-20) holds about 348 glyphs and is full, current translated text already needs 643 distinct syllables, and an unabridged script will need far more.

Item-name freeze is not an E6 misparse - 2026-07-31
- All 503 UI and item strings listed in `05_docs/ui_full_v42.csv` were decoded end to end. They contain zero control bytes in the `0xE2-0xE8` range and zero codes that index past the 409-entry lookup table.
- The same scan found 128 high-page glyph occurrences across 56 distinct virtual codes, and every one resolves to physical row 24. A `V == 32` test therefore covers exactly the P6 set in the current build.
- `05_docs/ui_glyph_store_v42_map.csv` is stale. It still records rows 32, 38 and 39 for 잎 책 탄 테 폴; the live EXE lookup was repacked to row 24 by v86.

COMM.IMG is a raw VRAM strip - measured 2026-07-31
- `COMM.IMG` is 458,752 bytes = 512 rows of 896 bytes, i.e. a 448 x 512 16bpp VRAM strip covering VRAM x4bpp 1280..3070, which is texture pages 5 through 11.
- Matching COMM.IMG content against a savestate gives `state_offset = 0x202058 + (o // 896) * 2048 + (o % 896)`. COMM rows map 1:1 onto VRAM rows; three independent probes matched exactly.
- The strip's left edge is VRAM x4bpp 1280, the tpage 5 boundary. Confirmed by decoding a live packet: the U=180 V=180 cell at x = 1280 + 180, y = 180 contains a clean 12x12 Hangul bitmap.
- Each 4bpp nibble holds four independent 1-bit glyphs. To inspect a glyph, isolate one bitplane; ORing all four looks like noise and will be mistaken for corruption.

The recorded P6 coordinates are strip-relative, not absolute - 2026-07-31
- `codex_notes` records the P6 area as "absolute texture area x=1576..1755". Those numbers are relative to the COMM.IMG strip, not absolute VRAM.
- Measured by diffing v85 against the current COMM.IMG, the v86 repack wrote the 57 glyphs at y = 288..298, x4bpp 2856..3033, and the destination was blank beforehand. 1576 + 1280 = 2856.
- x4bpp 2856 lies in texture page 11, which starts at 2816. 2856 - 2816 = 40, exactly the `addiu a3,a3,40` the glyph helper applies at `0x801FE410`. The U correction and the page number are two views of the same offset.
- Therefore the high-page tpage immediate is tx 11 plus the ty bit: `(base & 0xFFE0) | 0x1B`. v96 used `0x16` (tx 6) because it read the recorded numbers as absolute, and the sampler landed on unrelated texture data.
- Always measure a VRAM destination from the archives. A recorded coordinate whose frame of reference is not stated must be re-derived before it is used in a hook.

High-page tpage arithmetic - 2026-07-31
- For 4bpp, texture page N begins at VRAM x4bpp N*256. The tpage word holds tx in bits 0-3, ty in bit 4, abr in bits 5-6 and colour depth in bits 7-8.
- `andi a3,a3,0xFFE0` clears tx as well as ty. Preserving abr and colour depth while replacing both tx and ty is intentional, but the replacement must supply tx, not only the ty bit.
- The base tpage at `0x801F2FFC` is `0x0005` in 107 of 108 inspected savestates, so low-page glyphs live in page 5 and P6 in page 11. P6 pixels sit at a fixed VRAM location, so the high-page tx must be a constant and must not be derived as base+1.

Text-object struct is exactly 68 bytes with no spare room - 2026-07-31
- The initializer at `0x8016B134` takes a0 = slot limit, a1 = object address, a2 = packet base. It has 29 call sites but only four distinct object addresses: `0x801EFFBC` (limit 32), `0x801F1DB4` (16), `0x801F9D44` (128, one caller uses 64) and `0x801F9D88`.
- `0x801F9D44` and `0x801F9D88` are 68 bytes apart, so the struct is exactly 68 bytes.
- The initializer fills header fields `0x00-0x10`, then the loop at `0x8016B178` starts at s0 = 44 and steps by 12 while s0 < 68, building two 12-byte low-page `DR_TPAGE` primitives at `+44` and `+56`. Bytes 0..67 are therefore fully used.
- Conclusion: the two existing tpage slots are already committed to the low page, and the struct cannot hold two more for the high page. Do not plan on extending it.
- Memory layout per text object is `[packet array of limit*52 bytes][68-byte header]`, verified three times: `0x801EFC9C + 32*52 = 0x801F031C`, `0x801F0798 + 32*52 = 0x801F0E18`, `0x801F1A74 + 16*52 = 0x801F1DB4`. This is why writing at `base + 52*limit` landed exactly on the next object's header and froze the game.

Reserve the tpage slot in the renderer, not in the initializer - 2026-07-31
- v95 reserved the final packet slot by hooking the initializer and storing `limit - 1`. Correct, but global: every text object loses one glyph slot, so a string that exactly fills its buffer loses its last character.
- v98 moves the subtraction into the injected driver instead. The initializer is byte-identical to the original game again, and the driver computes `base + 52*(limit-1) + parity*20` itself. Both land on the same final slot.
- The driver rewrite is two words. The packet-base load moves up into the load-delay slot after `lh v0,0x4(s2)`, freeing its old slot for `addiu v1,v1,-52`:
    `0x801A2150` nop -> `lw s1,0x0(s2)`
    `0x801A2168` `lw s1,0x0(s2)` -> `addiu v1,v1,-52`
- Residual risk is now cosmetic and object-local: if a string fills all `limit` slots, the tpage shares the last slot with a glyph and that glyph draws wrong. It is no longer an out-of-bounds write.
- Longest translated string measured across `05_docs` is 58 slots, against a 128-slot dialogue object.

Terminology reference and its limits - 2026-07-31
- The scanned guidebook under `05_docs/참고사항/공략본/` is GAME MAGAZINE pp.88-96. Page 4 lists items and status effects, page 10 accessories and monsters, pages 5-8 the per-character skill tables, page 3 the stat names, pages 1-2 places and characters.
- Pages 4 and 10 print the Japanese original beside each Korean term, so those two tables are usable as a verified bilingual glossary. The skill tables on pages 5-8 do not, and they mostly transliterate the kanji instead of translating it.
- Reliability by category: items, accessories, stats, status effects and places are strong; character names are weaker because the guidebook uses non-standard transliteration; skill names are the weakest.
- Do not treat the guidebook as an authority. It is a 1990s magazine translation and it misreads the source in places. Confirmed misreadings: `幻のこて` rendered as 손끝 when 小手 is a forearm guard, `目つぶしの草` rendered as 모래 풀 when 目つぶし means blinding, `いやしのたてごと` rendered as 거문고 when 竪琴 is a harp, `荒獅子太鼓` rendered as 태고 when 太鼓 is simply a drum.
- Ruling order for terminology: source accuracy first, then consistency for proper nouns already used across the script, then common Korean JRPG usage when both readings are accurate.
- Glyph availability is not a reason to choose a word. Adopting every guidebook term would cost 13 new glyphs against 27 free slots on atlas row 24. Choose the wording on merit and add the glyph if the build reports it missing.
- The full decision record with per-term rulings is `05_docs/terminology_decisions.csv`, generated by `02_scripts/build_reference_glossary.py`. 134 terms: 29 already agree, 13 keep the current patch wording, 4 adopt the guidebook, 88 are guidebook-only terms not yet in the UI table.
- The four adopted from the guidebook are pure de-abbreviations that need no new glyphs: `回復果物` 회복 과실, `すばやさの薬` 민첩성의 약, `復活の薬` 부활의 약, `いのちの木の実` 생명나무의 열매.

Pending terminology change, deferred until regression testing completes - 2026-07-31
- Five UI strings are agreed but intentionally NOT applied yet. They wait until v98 has been checked over a wide play range, so that a terminology build does not add a variable on top of an only partly verified renderer.
- `回復果物` 회복 열매 -> 회복 과실
- `みなぎる果実` 넘치는 열매 -> 넘치는 과실
- `すばやさの薬` 민첩의 약 -> 민첩성의 약
- `復活の薬` 부활약 -> 부활의 약
- `いのちの木の実` 생명의 열매 -> 생명나무의 열매
- None of these need a new glyph, so the change is string replacement inside `PSX.EXE` only.
- Rule established while deciding them: the original distinguishes 果実/果物 from 実, and the translation now follows it. 果実/果物 -> 과실, 実 -> 열매, 葉 -> 잎, 泉 -> 샘. A full scan of the UI table found only these two entries had flattened the distinction; every 実, 葉 and 泉 entry was already correct.

LoadImage: available, and where it can be called - 2026-07-31
- Psy-Q `LoadImage` is linked at `0x80177E4C`. It takes a0 = RECT (short x, y, w, h in 16-bit units) and a1 = source pointer, and clamps to the 1024x512 VRAM bounds.
- It waits for the GPU itself: the loop at `0x80177FC4-0x80177FF0` spins on `GPUSTAT & 0x04000000` with a timeout call to `0x80178CCC`. Callers do not need their own synchronisation, but they must not call it while the GPU is busy.
- v99 hooked the text-render entry `0x8016B764` and deadlocked. The savestate showed `s6 = 0x80177E4C`, `ra = 0x80177FD0` and `s2 = 0x04000000`: the CPU sat in LoadImage's own wait loop while the frame it was blocking could never finish, so sprites stopped being drawn. Never call LoadImage from the render path.
- v100 hooked the frame loop instead and runs normally. The usable point is `0x8011C4AC`, the `jal 0x8011C814` that performs the frame swap. `0x8011C49C` calls the GPU driver's sync entry with a0 = 0 immediately before it, so the GPU is idle between the two.
- `0x8011C814` is the frame swap and the only writer of the buffer parity at render-context `+0x870`; roughly sixty other sites only read it. `0x801F12EC` holds the render-context pointer.

VRAM stability is not the same as invisibility - 2026-07-31
- The v100 probe wrote to VRAM x=208..223, y=468..479 (16-bit units), chosen because it never changed in any savestate. It is visible on the title screen, which uses a taller display area than later screens.
- A block being constant across savestates says nothing about whether it is displayed. Check the display area separately before assuming a scratch region is invisible.
- The corruption is harmless here and is in fact the visual confirmation that the transfer happened.

Heap is too small to host the glyph source - 2026-07-31
- Startup forms the heap start at `0x80175810` as `0x801FE4D8`, adds four, and passes it to `InitHeap` with size = initial SP minus `[0x801A7284]` minus that start.
- That leaves roughly 6-7 KB of heap between the reserved P6 helper and the stack. Taking 1 KB more is risky and 17 KB is impossible.
- A glyph source of that size has to live in the BSS region instead. `0x801CDE00-0x801D5BFF` (31.5 KB) never changed in any savestate, but it sits inside the startup BSS clear, so the bootstrap would have to clear around it rather than through it.
- Caveat on that measurement: the savestates represent only about 17 distinct loaded scenes, not 120 independent samples, so "never changed" is much weaker evidence than the file count suggests.

The BSS clear must stop at the relocated helper, not past it - 2026-07-31
- The original v85 bootstrap ended its clear loop at the BIOS memcpy return value, which is the destination pointer 0x801FE3C4. The relocated P6 helper therefore sat immediately above the cleared range and survived.
- 0x801FE4D8 is the helper's END and the v87 heap boundary. Using it as the clear endpoint zeroes the helper that was just copied. The first v101 build did exactly that; the savestate showed 0x801FE3C4 reading 00000000 instead of 2507FFE8, so the glyph hook ran into empty memory.
- Rule: the clear endpoint is HELPER_DST, never HELPER_DST + HELPER_N.
- `02_scripts/build_ui_hud_e7_v101_glyph_ram_upload.py` now refuses to build if the helper, the glyph block or the bootstrap overlaps any clear range, and checks that the two ranges plus the skipped block still cover the original span exactly.

Build a patch on the version that carries the code it depends on - 2026-07-31
- The first v101 was built on v98, which has neither the frame-boundary stub nor the hook that calls it; those arrived in v100. Only two words of a stub that did not exist were patched, so LoadImage was never called at all.
- v101 is now built on v100 and asserts that the stub prologue, its LoadImage call, its frame-swap tail call and the hook at 0x8011C4AC are all present before touching anything.
Measured original-disc script extraction boundary - 2026-08-01
- `E:\arc\원본\arc1.bin` SHA-256 is `16c62b4485bf29b97bc6090c58490cee718647624c3f4687b0aa4cdebae97c1a`.
- Its ISO9660 directory contains 256 `.DAT` files. The established `17/19` dialogue marker parser identifies 5,795 records across them and COMM.DAT (24 COMM.DAT records) when applied read-only to the original disc.
- The original disc's COMM.IMG SHA-256 equals `01_work/COMM.IMG`, so the current atlas-derived Japanese glyph map is based on the requested disc image.
- The parser/dictionary does NOT fully decode this corpus: 4,376 records include unresolved glyph or control markers. Do not claim that `05_docs/script_original_full.csv` is a complete Japanese transcription, and do not translate unresolved entries by inference.
- Existing `*translation*.csv` reconciliation at exact `(file, offset)` finds 655 unambiguous existing translations, 143 offsets with conflicting existing texts, and 4,997 without a translation. The 488 syllables in the generated requirement CSV are therefore only the unambiguous existing-translation subset, not a full-script requirement.

Original Japanese font-index mapping evidence boundary - 2026-08-01
- `05_docs/japanese_font_index_map.csv` has a row for every index 0..1239. Only 560 indices are `anchor`: each occurs in at least one source record that decodes with no unresolved glyph or control marker. No conflicting character was found among these anchors.
- No additional index was accepted from corpus inference or an external Japanese script in this pass. The public GameFAQs transcript located during research is English, so it cannot by itself prove a Japanese character at an encoded index.
- `E6 01` is a confirmed source-dialogue line break. `E4 1F` occurs at the end of repeated item-acquisition notices and is the parser's confirmed page/end break. Other `E4 xx` sequences remain unclassified control/escape candidates; do not split them into `E4` plus a glyph or assign them a visible character without format evidence.

Japanese font corpus propagation - 2026-08-01
- 106 additional index mappings were established only where a repeated normal-dialogue context or a fully constrained Japanese word fixed one character. They are tagged `corpus` with the actual context in `05_docs/japanese_font_index_map.csv`.
- Result: anchors=560, corpus=106, reference=0, unresolved=574; completely decoded extracted rows=1,843/5,795.
- High-frequency unresolved indices 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 14, 15, and 25 occur chiefly in parser candidates containing `00`/`FF` controls. They are not admissible corpus evidence until that record format is identified.

Rejected broad ASCII glyph rule - corrected 2026-08-01
- The earlier claim that atlas indices 0..94 equal ASCII code minus 32 was false. It extrapolated ten visual samples without checking the rest of the atlas, so the apparent 5,368-row decode count is invalid and must not be used for translation coverage or glyph-capacity planning.
- Direct extraction from the original disc's COMM.IMG (LBA 667; 12x12 4bpp plane per index) establishes the rule for indices 0..25. Index 0 is a blank ASCII space; 1..25 are `!` through `9`; 26..94 are Japanese/non-ASCII shapes. The evidence for every tested index is `05_docs/ascii_glyph_rule_audit.csv` and `01_work/analysis/ascii_glyph_audit/`.
- `script_original_full.csv` was first added in `2109797`, so no earlier CSV exists to restore. The repair instead proves every correction against the retained raw bytes: a raw index in 26..94 is changed only when its displayed token is exactly the unsupported ASCII candidate. It never assigns a Japanese character by inference: the token becomes `<G:n>`.
- The only actual substitutions are index 89 (256 occurrences) and index 90 (306 occurrences): 562 glyph positions in 277 source rows. The corpus now has 5,175/5,795 rows without `<G:`; 107 unresolved glyph indices occur 1,425 times. Rows with unresolved control markers remain a separate issue.
- Existing Korean text is restored exactly from task-start `32389d2`; no Korean wording is written or judged. The correction restores 111 cells that the mistaken index-0 repair had cleared, then clears 76 cells whose source row is genuinely restored to `<G:89>` or `<G:90>`. `apply_ascii_glyph_rule.py` now accepts only 0..25 and rejects the broad range so a future run cannot recreate the corruption.

Translation integrity repair - 2026-08-01
- `script_translated_full.csv` had 301 rows with a `<G:` marker in its Japanese cell and a non-empty Korean cell. These cells and their provenance labels were cleared, as a partial source string is not a translation target.
- `apply_script_translation_batch.py` is now safe to resume: completed `new` rows must match exactly, `existing` rows cannot be overwritten, and any planned row with `<G:` aborts. A rerun therefore only applies newly added, reviewed dictionary entries.
- After 34 additional reviewed dialogue lines, the canonical translated CSV contains 354 retained existing rows and 67 new rows. It remains incomplete and must not yet be used as the finished glyph-requirement measurement.

Translation batch identity rule - 2026-08-01
- `script_translated_full.csv` contains twelve duplicate `(source file, offset)` pairs in `COMM.DAT`; they are parser-generated non-dialogue candidates. A location alone is therefore not a stable mutation key.
- `apply_reviewed_translation_batch.py` uses the exact triple `(source file, normalized offset, Japanese source)` and requires it to match exactly one canonical row. It rejects blank sources, `<G:` sources, existing Korean text, duplicate batch triples, and a reused batch id before writing.
- Batch 20260801-001 was compared against frozen commit `67078e4`: exactly 33 canonical rows changed, all only in `korean` and provenance. This is the required verification pattern for every later translation batch.
Reviewed-batch newline notation - 2026-08-01
- Literal newlines in a quoted review CSV make a speaker label ending in a source-space look like a Git trailing-whitespace error. Review batches therefore represent embedded source line breaks as the two characters `\n`.
- `apply_reviewed_translation_batch.py` rejects physical CR/LF in the review-source field, decodes only literal `\n`, and compares the resulting source triple exactly. This preserves source spaces while keeping the review file one record per physical CSV line.
- Batch 20260801-002 changed exactly 19 canonical rows relative to commit `1fe4f69`; no pre-existing Korean or source cell changed.
Reviewed batch 20260801-003 - 2026-08-01
- The strict high-confidence screen allowed only 13 fully decoded `B/SB021`/`B/SB022` rows. Nearby rows with `<N:...>` markers were deliberately left blank even where surrounding context suggests their meaning.
- Relative to commit `2786d57`, batch 003 changed exactly 13 canonical rows and only their Korean/provenance fields.

Runtime text-token widths and CTRL00 false positives - 2026-08-01
- The original-disc `PSX.EXE` and `01_work/PSX.EXE` are byte-identical (SHA-256 `947ebf893f2d46207ec7e32ca514e4ea670e0bed34ef2144b5f7fb0fdd15bc67`). The parser evidence is therefore from the requested original executable, not a patched build.
- At `0x8016BB48`, bytes at or above E1 enter control preprocessing. `0x8016BBFC` reads the opcode, `0x8016BC08` reads its following argument, `0x8016BD40` returns token width 2, and `0x8016BC28` advances the stream by 2. `0x8016BBB4` terminates on a token-boundary zero.
- Established widths: `01..DC` one-byte glyphs; `DD..E0` two-byte glyphs; `E1..FF` two-byte opcode/argument pairs. Never decode `E6 00` as separate `<CTRL:E6>` and `<CTRL:00>` tokens.
- Confirmed visible controls remain `E6 01` newline and `E4 1F` page break. Other E1..FF pairs are width-confirmed but meaning-unconfirmed and must remain `<CTRL:XX:YY>`.
- The old parser searched for the first literal `00 00` without respecting token width. It could stop on the argument byte of `E6 00`, and it emitted every other zero as a standalone control. It also treated arbitrary aligned `17`/`19` values in numeric/layout data as record starts.
- Old corpus measurements at baseline `291ba49`: 5,795 rows, 5,783 unique locations, 3,027 CTRL00 rows in 179 files, 3,005 runtime-width-invalid affected rows, 2,392 mostly zero-high 16-bit rows, and 1,750 overlapping affected candidates. Ninety-six affected files also contain normal dialogue, so file identity alone cannot classify a row.
- Corrected corpus: 2,878 unique rows; 0 CTRL00; 713 rows retain 2,057 unclassified control pairs of 41 types. Their meanings were not guessed.
- The old table duplicated twelve locations in total because COMM.DAT was scanned both in the normal DAT list and by an explicit append. Four duplicated rows were complete plain text. The duplicate append is removed.
- Existing Korean is transferred only by exact `(source file, offset)`. All 2,024 non-empty Korean/provenance pairs survive unchanged; 528 of their Japanese display cells changed due correct token pairing and the prior atlas-map repair.
- `02_scripts/verify_ctrl00_parser_fix.py` pins the before state to commit `291ba49` and aborts if any accepted count, raw-byte match, translation preservation check, or representative sample changes.
- Remaining boundary: the `17/19` scanner is still a heuristic and is not proof that all game text formats are decoded. The purpose of this correction is only to reject impossible token streams and preserve real control pairs.

The stateless classifier still had a stateful renderer-entry gate - 2026-08-01
- v92 made pass selection stateless per packet, but it did not remove the earlier object-level sidecar trampoline. `0x8016B764` continued to jump to `0x801A22A0`; only a successful sidecar pointer/bitmap test there reaches the two-pass driver at `0x801A20B0`.
- The latest v115 state contains zero at all three relocated sidecar words. It therefore always takes the original-renderer fallback, even though the later packet classifier itself is stateless.
- The same state proves that physical glyph 3367 is not rejected: EA66 resolves to 3367 and the builder emits two item-name sprite packets with U=16, V=224 and plane 3. The missing artifact is the high-page `E100001F` DR_TPAGE primitive, not the glyph packet.
- The E9/EA decoder directly loads the selected lookup entry, and the common glyph builder divides that value by 84 without comparing it against a global table maximum. Do not infer an index ceiling from the largest unrelated value stored elsewhere in the lookup table.
- v108e retained a helper that could populate the old sidecar, while the rewritten two-strip helper no longer does. This explains why the same EA66-to-3367 mapping could render in v108e but not in v112/v115. v116 bypasses only the stale entry gate by changing `0x8016B764` from `j 0x801A22A0` to `j 0x801A20B0`.
Full 104-slot resident-store probe boundary - 2026-08-02
- User runtime testing accepted v116 for its exact target: the `잎` glyph renders and progression continues after bypassing the stale sidecar gate. Do not extend this result to the independent skill-range cursor regression.
- v116 has 104 physical resident positions but only 57 lookup entries pointed into them: 52 positions on row 40 and 5 positions on row 63. The other 47 positions were physically available but had not been exercised end to end.
- The early S1011 inline commands `E2 81` through `E2 84` select four external 0x80-byte secondary-text slots. At `0x8016BD94` that path calls the common glyph decoder `0x8016B3C0`; its hook at `0x8016B3D4` enters the E9/EA decoder at `0x801A74B8`. This statically establishes a controlled route for testing E9/EA virtual codes from early story text, but runtime acceptance is still required.
- v117 copies the current bitmap of each selected virtual character into exactly one resident position and rewrites that character's lookup entry to the copied position. It includes every one of the 57 pre-existing P6 users before adding 47 ordinary users, so no existing user is left pointing at overwritten test data.
- Test pages 1-2 cover all 52 positions in strip A; pages 3-4 cover all 52 positions in strip B. `01_work/analysis/ui_hud_e7_v117_104slot_story_probe/reference.png` is the visual answer sheet and `reference.csv` records every virtual code, source index and target index.
- This is a diagnostic build, not a translation build. It intentionally replaces the first four S1011 external messages and must not become the accepted baseline until all four pages match and the scene advances after page 4.

VRAM texture page 15,1 occupancy - 2026-08-02
- Measured over 99 save states. The game draws into exactly two blocks inside the page: y 268..287 across nearly the full width, and y 300..319 at x 960..999. Together 1207 halfwords.
- y 256..267 and y 320..479 are free in every state surveyed. The resident strips occupy y 480..491 and y 500..511.
- Nine save states are not enough to survey this page. Nine v118 states alone show it entirely free; the 88 older states are what reveal the two blocks. Any future free-space claim must state how many states it rests on.

Save state VRAM location - 2026-08-02
- The location of VRAM inside a save state cannot be found from the file's internal structure. An even-byte error is a pure horizontal shift of the whole image, and scoring alignment against texture page boundaries yields six candidates exactly one page (64 halfwords) apart.
- New format (`.sav`): accept only the (RAM base, VRAM base) pair for which the resident strips in reserved RAM equal, row for row, the VRAM rectangles they are uploaded to. Measured VRAM = GPU section + 689, RAM = Bus section + 64.
- Old format (`.state.bin`): no strips exist. Match hundreds of texture samples taken from an already-anchored VRAM; only one offset produces exact 16-byte matches. Measured VRAM = GPU section + 696.
- DuckStation `.sav` is a `DUCCT` header followed by a zstd frame at the offset stored at 0xC4, with the screenshot appended after the frame. Python 3.14's standard `compression.zstd` reads it. `02_scripts/extract_savestate_vram.py` does this.

Glyph strip placement rules - 2026-08-02
- The renderer emits one DR_TPAGE per text object at `0x801A2188..0x801A2198`, but U is written per glyph by the helper into packet byte `0x28`, and V per glyph as `(row * 12) & 0xFF`. A strip anywhere inside the page the objects already select therefore costs only one more row test in the helper, one more V test in the classifier, and one more LoadImage in the frame routine. A strip outside that page would need a tpage primitive per glyph.
- V must be a multiple of 4 and must avoid the 24 values the base rows produce: the multiples of 12 from 0 to 252, plus 8 and 20. The family V = 4 (mod 12) is clear, and its spacing of 12 matches the strip height so bands do not overlap.
- At x 960 the U offset is 0, so a band holds up to 21 columns (252 of the page's 256 pixels), which is 84 glyphs.
- Reserved RAM, not VRAM, is the binding limit: 18 bytes per glyph, 2520 bytes available above the v118 heap base, so at most 140 further glyphs without moving the heap again.

E9/EA lookup table - 2026-08-02
- The decoder at `0x801A74B8` computes `slot = 254 * (lead - 0xE9) + trail - 1` and indexes the table with no bounds check, so slots up to 507 are reachable.
- The table cannot grow at `0x801A7520`: the original executable's zero run ends at `0x801A7860` and real game data follows. Moving it costs two words, the `lui`/`ori` pair at `0x801A74E4` and `0x801A74E8`.
- Confirmed at runtime: a table appended to the reserved block, sourced from a sector appended to PSX.EXE, is carried intact by the boot memcpy. The appended sector only has to survive from the BIOS load until heap initialisation; it must not be used as the table's home, because that address range is live RAM once the game runs.

E9/EA lookup table is full - 2026-08-02
- v119 consumed slots 456..507. The decoder computes `254 * (lead - 0xE9) + trail - 1`, so 507 is the highest slot it can produce and the table is now completely allocated.
- A fourth strip therefore cannot be reached by adding a virtual code. Storage would exist but nothing could address it.
- The way out, if it is ever needed: roughly 300 syllables are stored twice, once at a physical code and once at a virtual E9/EA code pointing at a different index. Those virtual slots are redundant as long as the encoder emits the physical code for that syllable, so they can be repointed. That is an encoder and charmap change, not a renderer change, and it must be verified per syllable before any slot is reused.

Reserved-RAM routines have slack - 2026-08-02
- The helper allocated at `0x801FE3C4` is 276 bytes but v112 only used 56 of them, so extending its row tests costs nothing. v119's version is 68 bytes.
- The classifier and the frame routine sit at the end of the block, so growing them only shifts what v118 appended behind them. Strips A and B are ahead of them and keep their addresses through any such change.

Font-page free space: an empty plane is not an empty cell - 2026-08-02
- COMM.IMG stores four glyphs per 12x12 cell, one per bitplane, but the GPU reads a 4bpp pixel as one CLUT index built from all four planes at once. Writing a glyph into a spare plane of a cell that still holds art does not add a glyph beside it; it recolours the art.
- Font-page index 1671 (row 19, column 18) looked free because plane 3 was clear across the whole cell. It holds the START button icon in planes 0..2. v118 wrote there and the icon changed colour in game.
- Any check for a free font-page slot must test the whole cell across all four planes, never the target plane alone. `cell_is_occupied` in `build_ui_hud_e7_v118_strip_b_fill.py` does this.
- This is the third time the project has made this mistake: v109 and v111 broke glyphs the same way. Treat the 77 "blank planes in cells that still hold art" as permanently unavailable.

Diagnosing a visual regression: get the build boundary first - 2026-08-02
- When the START icon was reported, I reasoned from the newest change (v119's third strip and its new classifier V) and built a detailed and wrong theory around it. The user then compared v116 against v118 and the real boundary was immediately obvious.
- Establishing which build first shows a symptom costs one comparison and removes whole branches of speculation. Do it before analysing mechanism.

Insertion capacity is a separate problem from the glyph store - 2026-08-02
- The glyph work answers which syllables can be drawn. It does not answer how much text fits, and the second question is now the binding one.
- Story insertion is an in-place overwrite. `build_story_*.py` refuses any line whose encoding exceeds the Japanese bytes it replaces, so every line's budget is set by the sentence it is replacing.
- Measured across all 2,650 translated lines against v119, with the character-to-code table read out of the built archive: 371 lines (14%) fit, 2,279 (86%) do not. Total overflow 37,131 bytes, average 16 bytes per line. `02_scripts/audit_insertion_budget_v119.py`, results in `01_work/analysis/insertion_budget_v119.txt` and `insertion_overflow_v119.csv`.
- The E2 external-slot mechanism absorbs this. A file has 79 slots of 128 bytes, per the expanded slot map of 2026-07-16 recorded above, and 387 bodies already ship through it.
- Against 79 slots: 151 of the 152 affected files fit. One does not -- C2/SC0B6.DAT needs 98 slots for 1,059 bytes, so about 19 of its lines have to lose roughly 11 bytes each to stay inline. That is the entire remaining editorial burden.
- Bytes were never close: the hungriest file needs 1,439 of the 10,112 a full bank holds.
- CORRECTION. The first version of this note said 16 slots and concluded that 48 files were beyond reach, implying 2,279 lines would need trimming. That was wrong. The 16 figure came from the v117/v120 probe constants, which allocate only the first few slots, and from a superseded test_log entry. The expansion to 79 was already recorded in this file. Read forward from a mechanism's first appearance to its latest revision before quoting a capacity.

E2 external slot mechanism, consolidated - 2026-08-02
- Assembled from notes scattered across codex_notes, test_log and changelog because quoting a partial version of this produced a wrong capacity claim twice in one session. Read this entry, not the older fragments.

- Layout. A scene file reserves 0x45000..0x477FF, ahead of dialogue bodies that begin near 0x47800. That is 79 complete 128-byte slots. Slot n is at file offset 0x45000 + n * 0x80. The file-to-RAM delta is +0xCF000, so slot 0 is RAM 0x80114000; the address formula is 0x80114000 + slot * 0x80, and the earlier 0x80110000 + slot * 0x80 was wrong because it omitted the 0x4000 bank displacement.

- Disk IDs. 81-A8 select slots 0..39 and AA-D0 select slots 40..78. **A9 is reserved: the original parsed dialogue already uses it.** So the 80 IDs in 81..D0 yield 79 slots, not 80. This is the one that goes missing from the expansion.
- The lookup receives the decremented internal ID. Low slots map internal - 0x80, high slots map internal - 0x81; completion maps disk - 0x81 or disk - 0x82. The one-step shift across A9 must be applied in both the lookup and the completion path, or the slot resolves one place off.

- Per-slot cost. Byte 0x7F of each slot holds the completion metadata, `original capacity - 2`, and the text is zero-terminated before it. Usable text is therefore under 128 bytes per slot, not 128.

- What cannot go in a slot. `E6 01` is not interpreted inside a secondary string and renders as visible garbage; use spaces and let the renderer wrap. The four S3032 blocks at 0x45000..0x45180 each carried two `E6 01` and showed a garbled glyph at exactly those positions.
- A body that mixes prose with `E5` choices must not use `capacity - 2` metadata, because that skip would swallow the choices. For S3012 0x47FF0, E2 replaces only the prompt and the option text stays inline within its original capacity.

- Allocation is per file, not global. Some scene files use part of the nominal bank for real data, so a builder must test each 128-byte slot in the actual cumulative file and take only fully zero ones. S1072's sixteen were empty; that is a measurement of one file, not a rule.

- Separately, the battle-icon work removed capacity of a different kind. Relocating the E7 confirm/cancel icons consumed two charmap-unassigned 12x12 font cells, and the recorded canaries that later builds must not disturb are the v0.39 0x6C LV plane, the E7 icon rectangles, the battle-cursor region, five HUD special payloads, 17 Japanese-preserved rows, and all story members. E7 icons sit at V=130 and, after relocation, IDs 2 and 3 at U=180/192 with V=228. Any classifier that tests V by exact value must avoid 130 and 228; a `V % 12` test would misclassify them.

v121 bulk insertion regressed the row geometry - 2026-08-02
- Runtime: vertical text drift, choices off by a row, the cursor covering an option, and a speaker label alone on the first row. All of it is one cause.
- v121 gave every relocated body a completion skip of `capacity - 2`. 1,292 of its 1,800 E2 writes therefore skip over the body's own E6 line breaks. The rule against exactly this is recorded above under "External choice prompt row rule - 2026-07-17": never skip a body that contains E6, because the renderer's current row and the menu cursor's parsed row then diverge.
- The planner excluded E5 bodies and did not exclude E6 bodies. E5 was the visible half of the rule; E6 was the half that mattered here.
- The documented repair is one E2 call per non-empty source text row, each slot's skip ending immediately before that row's original E6, so the inline parser still executes the break. For a single-row prompt followed by E6, keep one command and shorten the skip to `row_end - row_start - 2`.
- That repair cannot be applied mechanically to this corpus. Of the 1,292 affected bodies only 14 have a `|` count in the Korean matching the source E6 count; 1,278 disagree, and 1,277 of those are Korean with no `|` at all against a source of one to six rows. The translation was written as flowing sentences, so the row structure it must be poured back into no longer exists in it.
- The 508 bodies with no E6 in the source were always safe and are the only part of v121 that can be kept as built.
- Separately: untranslated Japanese still on screen is not a font fault. The font keeps 17 Japanese rows by design, and any body not yet replaced renders from them. The reported line, `32/S3051.DAT 0x48D62`, is translated in the table but was one of the 484 the planner left out.

Put rules in the builder, not only in this file - 2026-08-02
- Four regressions in one session repeated something already written down: the blank-plane trap (v109, v111, then v118 and the START icon), the E2 slot count, the E6 skip rule, and the instruction to find the first build that shows a symptom before theorising. In every case the answer was in the documents and the build was made first.
- A rule kept only as prose depends on someone reading it at the right moment, and reading it far enough. Both failed here, twice each.
- Where a rule can be checked mechanically, the builder should check it and refuse. `cell_is_occupied` in the v118 builder now makes the blank-plane mistake unbuildable; the E6 guard in `plan_bulk_insertion.py` does the same for the skip rule. Prefer adding a guard over adding a paragraph.
- A guard is also self-documenting at the moment it matters: it fails with the reason, at the point of the mistake, instead of waiting for a screenshot.

A rule stops new defects; it does not remove old ones - 2026-08-02
- `E6 01` inside an E2 secondary string is drawn rather than obeyed and shows as one squashed glyph mid-sentence. That was established 2026-07-15 and every builder since has honoured it.
- The archive still carried 38 slots holding 54 of them, written before the rule existed. They were still on screen in v119. The user found one in `32/S3051.DAT` slot 63, between the fourth and fifth syllables of a sentence, and recognised it as a defect that had already cost a lot of work.
- When a rule is added, sweep the data that was written without it. A guard protects the future; the past needs a migration. `02_scripts/build_story_v122_strip_e6_from_slots.py` is that sweep.
- Writing the sweep hit the trap the rule warns about in its own right: scanning for the byte 0xE6 rather than for the token `E6 01` found 24 false positives, because 0xE6 also occurs as the second byte of a two-byte glyph. Walk the stream -- 0x01..0xDC one byte, 0xDD.. two bytes -- and judge a byte only at a token boundary.

Glyph index 179 was ベ on a guess; it is 恵 - 2026-08-02
- The map recorded 179 as the katakana ベ, established as "anchor: appears in a completely decoded corpus string" -- inferred from context, never checked against the glyph.
- Its bitmap is 53 pixels with a 心 radical under a boxed upper half. ベ is two strokes and would be three or four pixels. The cell is 恵.
- So `ベみの精霊` was `恵みの精霊`, a spirit of blessing, and 베미 was never a name. 57 lines carried it; 53 now read 은혜의 정령.
- This is the third map entry found wrong this way, after 208 (ニ for コ). The pattern is the same: an entry justified by context rather than by pixels. Treat "established from context" as provisional and check the bitmap before trusting any name built on it.
- A reader's review caught this one. The reasoning that found it needs no Japanese: `ベみ` mixes katakana and hiragana inside one word, which real Japanese does not do. A mixed-script word in the decoded corpus is a signal that a glyph is misread.

Verifying a build somebody else made - 2026-08-04
- Check the claim against the artifact, not against the report. Two claims in the v127/v128 reports did not survive that: the rect table appears to hold only three strips because the fourth rect lives in reserved RAM at 0x801FF834, and the classifier at 0x801FEC28 still tests three V values because the live one was relocated to 0x801FE420. Both looked like failures until the call sites were followed.
- Follow the call site before concluding a routine is stale. `0x801A2204` names the classifier and `0x8016B5D8` the helper; a copy left at an old address proves nothing either way.
- The v128 builder finds E5 and E6 by scanning raw bytes rather than walking tokens. Re-checked all 63 bodies both ways and they agreed, so nothing is wrong in this build, but the same shortcut has cost this project three builds elsewhere. Harden it before the next body set.
- 못 lives at physical E0 F5 / index 1229 and always did. Index 1338 holds the superseded hand-drawn 큐, and lookup slot 98 still points there. Do not repeat the claim that 못 and 큐 share 1338.

Relocation needs a coverage check, not a read-back check - 2026-08-04
- When glyphs are moved out of a region so the region can be restored, verifying that the moved ones read back at their new address proves nothing about the ones that were missed. Enumerate the region first, then require every live plane in it to have a destination.
- v127 moved 46 planes out of the skill-range cells and left 러 and 뜨 behind. Both had ink in v126, both are common, and 뜨 had no second home, so it became undrawable. 534 occurrences in the shipped archive.
- The check that finds this: for each plane in the region, does the v126 bitmap name a character, and does the remap table give it a destination? A character on one side and FF on the other is the defect.

The strip D remap ABI, and how to add to it - 2026-08-05
- The remap table at `0x801FF7F4` is 64 bytes, one per plane of the 16 restored skill-range cells, indexed `(row-10)*16 + (col-2)*4 + plane`. 0xFF means leave the cell alone; anything else is a strip D slot.
- The lookup at `0x801FE4A4` returns `slot + 4368`. 4368 is 52*84, so the slot is simply re-expressed as row 52 of the font page and every downstream U/V calculation is the ordinary one. There is no per-strip special case to update when adding a glyph.
- A strip is 936 bytes, 78 per row, which is 156 4bpp pixels: 13 columns of 12, so 52 slots as `col*4 + plane`. The rects confirm it -- strip D's is (961, 368, 39, 12) and 39 halfwords is those same 156 pixels. Column 12 is real and strips A, B and C all use it.
- So adding a glyph to a strip is two writes: the 12x12 plane into a free slot, and the slot number into the table. Take the bitmap from the last build that still held it rather than re-rendering, so the diff stays provably minimal.

The font spans two texture pages, and only the classifier can cross - 2026-08-05
- The font sits at VRAM page x=5 and runs from y=0 to y=467, so font rows 0..21 are on texture page (5,0) and rows 22..39 are on (5,1). V is `(row*12) & 0xFF` and is correct for both halves; what distinguishes them is the texture page, and the texture page is chosen per glyph by the classifier.
- The classifier only recognises the four resident strips by their V values. It has no test for "ordinary font row on the second page", so every non-strip glyph on rows 22..39 draws from page (5,0) at the same U and V: right position on screen, wrong twelve rows of pixels. On screen it reads as one character vertically smeared into its neighbour.
- This is why the V selection rule for a new strip says to avoid 8 and 20: those are rows 22 and 23, the first two rows of the second page.
- The encoder must never hand out a physical code whose row is 22..39 unless that row is a strip. Prefer the strip code; every one of the 25 characters that had this problem already had a correct twin in strip A. Put the check in `build_encoder`, not in a comment.
- How to prove a glyph defect of this shape in one step: take the 12x12 ink mask off the framebuffer and search the whole VRAM for it. One exact hit gives the page, U and V the renderer actually used, and comparing that against the glyph's real location says whether the fault is U, V, the page, or the data.

An external slot holds 126 bytes of text, not 127 - 2026-08-05
- The slot is 128 bytes: text, a 0x00 terminator, then the `capacity - 2` completion-skip metadata in byte 0x7F. A 127-byte payload leaves no room for the terminator, and the metadata write then lands on byte 127 anyway, so the string never ends and the renderer walks into the next slot. It hangs.
- `SLOT_TEXT_MAX` must be 126. It was 127 and seven slots shipped without a terminator.
- The guard to add is not on the payload length but on the result: after writing a slot, require a 0x00 in bytes 0..126. Length arithmetic is what got this wrong in the first place.

An E2 slot inherits the original line's window, and cannot grow it - 2026-08-06
- The dialogue window is 21 cells wide, and how many rows it has is decided by the line breaks in the ORIGINAL body, not by the replacement text. A slot whose Korean needs more rows than the original had draws until the window is full and then stops; the game does not recover.
- So the budget for an E2 line is `(original E6 01 count + 1) * 21` cells, counting every glyph and every space as one cell. Check it in the builder and show it in the editor; it is a harder limit than the 126-byte slot size and it bites first.
- E6 inside a slot is drawn, not interpreted, so line breaks cannot be used to control the wrap. Shortening the text is the only lever.
- Save state 6 froze on `21/S2041.DAT 0x485A6`: three rows of window, 66 cells of Korean, 63 cells drawn.

Do not write Korean into a font cell that already has ink - 2026-08-06
